Executive Summary  \n    10→2. Layered Architecture  \n    11→   2.1. High-Level Component Map  \n    12→   2.2. Build & Deployment Model  \n    13→3. Core Runtime Patterns  \n    14→   3.1. Strategy Pattern – Transform & Validate  \n    15→   3.2. Event-Streaming Bus  \n    16→   3.3. ETL & Pipeline Pattern  \n    17→   3.4. Data-Lake Façade  \n    18→   3.5. Pseudo-Microservices Layer  \n    19→   3.6. Observer Pattern – Telemetry & Alerting  \n    20→4. Threading & Concurrency Model  \n    21→5. Data Security & Compliance  \n    22→6. Extensibility Points  \n    23→7. Failure Domains & Error-Handling Strategy  \n    24→8. Directory Layout  \n    25→9. Appendix  \n    26→   9.1. Example Strategy Plugin (C++17)  \n    27→   9.2. Sample Runtime Config (YAML)  \n    28→\n    29→---\n    30→\n    31→## 1. Executive Summary\n    32→CardioInsight360 is a **single, self-contained C++17 binary** that delivers high-volume cardiovascular analytics in both near-real-time *and* batch modes.  \n    33→The monolithic approach keeps operational complexity low for hospital IT while still **internally** applying well-known distributed-systems patterns:\n    34→\n    35→* **Strategy Pattern** enables plug-and-play data-quality & transformation policies.  \n    36→* **Apache Kafka** (via `librdkafka`) provides a low-latency in-process **Event-Streaming bus**.  \n    37→* An **ETL Pipeline** leverages **Intel TBB** for data-parallel execution.  \n    38→* A **Data-Lake Façade** abstracts hierarchical Parquet storage behind a simple C++ API.  \n    39→* A lightweight **pseudo-Microservices** layer offers logical service boundaries without extra deployables.  \n    40→* **Observer Pattern** exposes health metrics to Prometheus-compatible collectors.\n    41→\n    42→Result: hospital networks deploy *one* artifact yet benefit from sophisticated, horizontally-scalable analytics.\n    43→\n    44→---\n    45→\n    46→## 2. Layered Architecture\n    47→\n    48→### 2.1. High-Level Component Map\n    49→\n    50→```mermaid\n    51→flowchart TD\n    52→    subgraph Ingestion\n    53→        HL7[HL7 / FHIR Listener] --> DeMux{Message Demultiplexer}\n    54→    end\n    55→\n    56→    subgraph StreamingBus\n    57→        DeMux -->|ECG Stream| Kafka[(Embedded\\nKafka Broker)]\n    58→        DeMux -->|Vitals Stream| Kafka\n    59→    end\n    60→\n    61→    subgraph ETL\n    62→        Kafka -->|Batch Window| ETLWorker\n    63→        ETLWorker --> DataLakeFaçade\n    64→    end\n    65→\n    66→    subgraph Services\n    67→        ETLWorker --> Scheduler\n    68→        ETLWorker --> Visualizer\n    69→        ETLWorker --> ErrorSvc\n    70→    end\n    71→\n    72→    DataLakeFaçade --> DASH[Dashboards & Research APIs]\n    73→```\n    74→\n    75→Key: everything executes **inside the same process space**; inter-service calls are in-memory function calls rather than RPC.\n    76→\n    77→### 2.2. Build & Deployment Model\n    78→* **Build**: CMake with presets for *Release*, *Instrumented*, *FIPS-Compliant*.  \n    79→* **Artifact**: single ELF/PE/Mach-O binary (`cardioinsight360`) + optional CLI helper.  \n    80→* **Dependencies** are statically linked where licenses permit (e.g., `librdkafka`, `libpqxx`, `parquet-arrow`).\n    81→\n    82→---\n    83→\n    84→## 3. Core Runtime Patterns\n    85→\n    86→### 3.1. Strategy Pattern – Transform & Validate\n    87→\n    88→Each physiological signal type (ECG, BP, SpO₂) registers a `TransformationStrategy` at runtime.  \n    89→Contracts:\n    90→\n    91→```cpp\n    92→struct TransformationStrategy {\n    93→    virtual std::string schema_id() const = 0;\n    94→    virtual ValidatedRecord process(const RawRecord&) = 0;\n    95→    virtual ~TransformationStrategy() = default;\n    96→};\n    97→```\n    98→\n    99→The engine auto-discovers shared libraries in `<prefix>/lib/strategies/*.so` and installs them into a map keyed by `schema_id()`.\n   100→\n   101→### 3.2. Event-Streaming Bus\n   102→* Embeds a **single-node Kafka** broker (configured for zero-replication, log-compaction) to avoid external infra.  \n   103→* Producers & consumers live in the same process yet adhere to Kafka semantics → switch to real cluster by config only.\n   104→\n   105→### 3.3. ETL & Pipeline Pattern\n   106→* **Staged**: `ExtractStage -> TransformStage -> LoadStage`.  \n   107→* **Parallel**: per stage and per patient/time-window using **Intel TBB Flow Graph**.  \n   108→* **Batch & Real-Time** share code; *real-time* uses tiny windows (2 s) vs *batch* (24 h).\n   109→\n   110→### 3.4. Data-Lake Façade\n   111→```cpp\n   112→class DataLake {\n   113→public:\n   114→    WriteHandle open_writer(const PartitionKey&);\n   115→    ReadHandle  open_reader(const PartitionSelection&);\n   116→};\n   117→```\n   118→* Physical storage back-end: local FS, NFS, or S3 (via `aws-sdk-cpp`).  \n   119→* Layout: `/modality=ecg/year=2024/month=06/day=01/patient_id=<uuid>.parquet`.\n   120→\n   121→### 3.5. Pseudo-Microservices Layer\n   122→* Scheduler, Visualization, and Error-Recovery each sit behind an interface enabling independent release cycles, yet are compiled in.  \n   123→* Interactions occur via C++20 **Sender/Receiver** channels, not gRPC.\n   124→\n   125→### 3.6. Observer Pattern – Telemetry & Alerting\n   126→* All major subsystems derive from `Observable`; metrics are pushed to a central `MetricsRegistry`.  \n   127→* A Prometheus exporter scrapes `localhost:9191/metrics`.\n   128→\n   129→---\n   130→\n   131→## 4. Threading & Concurrency Model\n   132→* **TBB Task Scheduler** governs fine-grained tasking.  \n   133→* Thread-safe singletons are avoided; DI container hands out strictly-scoped instances.  \n   134→* Critical sections guarded by `std::shared_mutex`; no global `mutex`es.  \n   135→* Hot paths (e.g., ECG R-peak detection) use lock-free ring buffers (`folly::ProducerConsumerQueue`).\n   136→\n   137→---\n   138→\n   139→## 5. Data Security & Compliance\n   140→1. HIPAA AES-256 encryption at rest via LUKS or KMS-managed keys (S3).  \n   141→2. TLS 1.3 for any remote connectivity (optional).  \n   142→3. PHI tokenization configurable per facility.  \n   143→4. Role-based access controlled **at CLI layer**; no privilege escalation inside process.  \n   144→\n   145→---\n   146→\n   147→## 6. Extensibility Points\n   148→| Extension Type | Mechanism | Loading |\n   149→| -------------- | --------- | ------- |\n   150→| Transformation Strategy | Shared-lib + C++17 ABI | `dlopen()`/`LoadLibrary` |\n   151→| Custom Alert Rules | Embedded Lua 5.4 | Run-time |\n   152→| Storage Back-End | Adapter pattern | Compile-time flag |\n   153→\n   154→---\n   155→\n   156→## 7. Failure Domains & Error-Handling Strategy\n   157→* **Brown-out > Black-out**: degrade to batch-only mode if Kafka is unstable.  \n   158→* **Circuit Breakers** guard external systems (e.g., PACS).  \n   159→* **Replay**: deterministic offsets + idempotent writes enable at-least-once semantics.  \n   160→* **Crash Resilience**: fully stateless; restart and resume by reading last committed offsets from Data-Lake manifest.\n   161→\n   162→---\n   163→\n   164→## 8. Directory Layout\n   165→```\n   166→cardio_insight_360/\n   167→├─ CMakeLists.txt\n   168→├─ src/\n   169→│  ├─ core/                  # Domain models & value objects\n   170→│  ├─ strategies/            # Built-in transformation plugins\n   171→│  ├─ services/              # Pseudo-microservices\n   172→│  └─ ui/                    # Qt 6 dashboards\n   173→├─ third_party/\n   174→├─ data/                     # Sample datasets (git-lfs)\n   175→└─ docs/\n   176→   ├─ architecture/README.md # <— you are here\n   177→   └─ api/\n   178→```\n   179→\n   180→---\n   181→\n   182→## 9. Appendix\n   183→\n   184→### 9.1. Example Strategy Plugin (C++17)\n   185→\n   186→```cpp\n   187→// File: plugins/strategies/ArrhythmiaValidator.cpp\n   188→#include \"core/TransformationStrategy.hpp\"\n   189→#include \"domain/ecg/BeatClassifier.hpp\"\n   190→#include <spdlog/spdlog.h>\n   191→\n   192→class ArrhythmiaValidator final : public TransformationStrategy {\n   193→public:\n   194→    std::string schema_id() const override { return \"ecg.v1\"; }\n   195→\n   196→    ValidatedRecord process(const RawRecord& raw) override {\n   197→        auto beats = BeatClassifier::detect_beats(raw.payload);\n   198→        if (!beats.valid()) {\n   199→            throw ValidationError{\"Arrhythmia check failed\"};\n   200→        }\n   201→        ValidatedRecord out;\n   202→        out.patient_id  = raw.patient_id;\n   203→        out.timestamp   = raw.timestamp;\n   204→        out.cleaned_electrodes = beats.cleaned_signal();\n   205→        return out;\n   206→    }\n   207→};\n   208→\n   209→extern \"C\" TransformationStrategy* create_plugin() {\n   210→    return new ArrhythmiaValidator{};\n   211→}\n   212→```\n   213→\n   214→> Compile as:  \n   215→> `g++ -std=c++17 -fPIC -shared ArrhythmiaValidator.cpp -o libarrhythmia_validator.so`\n   216→\n   217→### 9.2. Sample Runtime Config (`config.yaml`)\n   218→```yaml\n   219→ingestion:\n   220→  hl7_port: 2575\n   221→  fhir_port: 8080\n   222→  kafka_socket: inproc://kafka\n   223→\n   224→storage:\n   225→  backend: \"filesystem\"\n   226→  root_path: \"/var/lib/cardioinsight360/datalake\"\n   227→\n   228→security:\n   229→  encryption_at_rest: true\n   230→  tls:\n   231→    enabled: false\n   232→\n   233→services:\n   234→  scheduler:\n   235→    max_parallel_jobs: 8\n   236→  visualization:\n   237→    port: 9000\n   238→  error_recovery:\n   239→    retry_backoff_ms: 500\n   240→\n   241→strategies_path: \"/opt/cardioinsight360/plugins/strategies\"\n   242→```\n   243→\n   244→---\n   245→\n   246→© 2024 CardioInsight360 Contributors. Executive Summary  \n2. Layered Architecture  \n   2.1. High-Level Component Map  \n   2.2. Build & Deployment Model  \n3. Core Runtime Patterns  \n   3.1. Strategy Pattern – Transform & Validate  \n   3.2. Event-Streaming Bus  \n   3.3. ETL & Pipeline Pattern  \n   3.4. Data-Lake Façade  \n   3.5. Pseudo-Microservices Layer  \n   3.6. Observer Pattern – Telemetry & Alerting  \n4. Threading & Concurrency Model  \n5. Data Security & Compliance  \n6. Extensibility Points  \n7. Failure Domains & Error-Handling Strategy  \n8. Directory Layout  \n9. Appendix  \n   9.1. Example Strategy Plugin (C++17)  \n   9.2. Sample Runtime Config (YAML)  \n\n---\n\n## 1. Executive Summary\nCardioInsight360 is a **single, self-contained C++17 binary** that delivers high-volume cardiovascular analytics in both near-real-time *and* batch modes.  \nThe monolithic approach keeps operational complexity low for hospital IT while still **internally** applying well-known distributed-systems patterns:\n\n* **Strategy Pattern** enables plug-and-play data-quality & transformation policies.  \n* **Apache Kafka** (via `librdkafka`) provides a low-latency in-process **Event-Streaming bus**.  \n* An **ETL Pipeline** leverages **Intel TBB** for data-parallel execution.  \n* A **Data-Lake Façade** abstracts hierarchical Parquet storage behind a simple C++ API.  \n* A lightweight **pseudo-Microservices** layer offers logical service boundaries without extra deployables.  \n* **Observer Pattern** exposes health metrics to Prometheus-compatible collectors.\n\nResult: hospital networks deploy *one* artifact yet benefit from sophisticated, horizontally-scalable analytics.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Layered Architecture\n\n### 2.1. High-Level Component Map\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart TD\n    subgraph Ingestion\n        HL7[HL7 / FHIR Listener] --> DeMux{Message Demultiplexer}\n    end\n\n    subgraph StreamingBus\n        DeMux -->|ECG Stream| Kafka[(Embedded\\nKafka Broker)]\n        DeMux -->|Vitals Stream| Kafka\n    end\n\n    subgraph ETL\n        Kafka -->|Batch Window| ETLWorker\n        ETLWorker --> DataLakeFaçade\n    end\n\n    subgraph Services\n        ETLWorker --> Scheduler\n        ETLWorker --> Visualizer\n        ETLWorker --> ErrorSvc\n    end\n\n    DataLakeFaçade --> DASH[Dashboards & Research APIs]\n```\n\nKey: everything executes **inside the same process space**; inter-service calls are in-memory function calls rather than RPC.\n\n### 2.2. Build & Deployment Model\n* **Build**: CMake with presets for *Release*, *Instrumented*, *FIPS-Compliant*.  \n* **Artifact**: single ELF/PE/Mach-O binary (`cardioinsight360`) + optional CLI helper.  \n* **Dependencies** are statically linked where licenses permit (e.g., `librdkafka`, `libpqxx`, `parquet-arrow`).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Core Runtime Patterns\n\n### 3.1. Strategy Pattern – Transform & Validate\n\nEach physiological signal type (ECG, BP, SpO₂) registers a `TransformationStrategy` at runtime.  \nContracts:\n\n```cpp\nstruct TransformationStrategy {\n    virtual std::string schema_id() const = 0;\n    virtual ValidatedRecord process(const RawRecord&) = 0;\n    virtual ~TransformationStrategy() = default;\n};\n```\n\nThe engine auto-discovers shared libraries in `<prefix>/lib/strategies/*.so` and installs them into a map keyed by `schema_id()`.\n\n### 3.2. Event-Streaming Bus\n* Embeds a **single-node Kafka** broker (configured for zero-replication, log-compaction) to avoid external infra.  \n* Producers & consumers live in the same process yet adhere to Kafka semantics → switch to real cluster by config only.\n\n### 3.3. ETL & Pipeline Pattern\n* **Staged**: `ExtractStage -> TransformStage -> LoadStage`.  \n* **Parallel**: per stage and per patient/time-window using **Intel TBB Flow Graph**.  \n* **Batch & Real-Time** share code; *real-time* uses tiny windows (2 s) vs *batch* (24 h).\n\n### 3.4. Data-Lake Façade\n```cpp\nclass DataLake {\npublic:\n    WriteHandle open_writer(const PartitionKey&);\n    ReadHandle  open_reader(const PartitionSelection&);\n};\n```\n* Physical storage back-end: local FS, NFS, or S3 (via `aws-sdk-cpp`).  \n* Layout: `/modality=ecg/year=2024/month=06/day=01/patient_id=<uuid>.parquet`.\n\n### 3.5. Pseudo-Microservices Layer\n* Scheduler, Visualization, and Error-Recovery each sit behind an interface enabling independent release cycles, yet are compiled in.  \n* Interactions occur via C++20 **Sender/Receiver** channels, not gRPC.\n\n### 3.6. Observer Pattern – Telemetry & Alerting\n* All major subsystems derive from `Observable`; metrics are pushed to a central `MetricsRegistry`.  \n* A Prometheus exporter scrapes `localhost:9191/metrics`.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Threading & Concurrency Model\n* **TBB Task Scheduler** governs fine-grained tasking.  \n* Thread-safe singletons are avoided; DI container hands out strictly-scoped instances.  \n* Critical sections guarded by `std::shared_mutex`; no global `mutex`es.  \n* Hot paths (e.g., ECG R-peak detection) use lock-free ring buffers (`folly::ProducerConsumerQueue`).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Data Security & Compliance\n1. HIPAA AES-256 encryption at rest via LUKS or KMS-managed keys (S3).  \n2. TLS 1.3 for any remote connectivity (optional).  \n3. PHI tokenization configurable per facility.  \n4. Role-based access controlled **at CLI layer**; no privilege escalation inside process.  \n\n---\n\n## 6. Extensibility Points\n| Extension Type | Mechanism | Loading |\n| -------------- | --------- | ------- |\n| Transformation Strategy | Shared-lib + C++17 ABI | `dlopen()`/`LoadLibrary` |\n| Custom Alert Rules | Embedded Lua 5.4 | Run-time |\n| Storage Back-End | Adapter pattern | Compile-time flag |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Failure Domains & Error-Handling Strategy\n* **Brown-out > Black-out**: degrade to batch-only mode if Kafka is unstable.  \n* **Circuit Breakers** guard external systems (e.g., PACS).  \n* **Replay**: deterministic offsets + idempotent writes enable at-least-once semantics.  \n* **Crash Resilience**: fully stateless; restart and resume by reading last committed offsets from Data-Lake manifest.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Directory Layout\n```\ncardio_insight_360/\n├─ CMakeLists.txt\n├─ src/\n│  ├─ core/                  # Domain models & value objects\n│  ├─ strategies/            # Built-in transformation plugins\n│  ├─ services/              # Pseudo-microservices\n│  └─ ui/                    # Qt 6 dashboards\n├─ third_party/\n├─ data/                     # Sample datasets (git-lfs)\n└─ docs/\n   ├─ architecture/README.md # <— you are here\n   └─ api/\n```\n\n---\n\n## 9. Appendix\n\n### 9.1. Example Strategy Plugin (C++17)\n\n```cpp\n// File: plugins/strategies/ArrhythmiaValidator.cpp\n#include \"core/TransformationStrategy.hpp\"\n#include \"domain/ecg/BeatClassifier.hpp\"\n#include <spdlog/spdlog.h>\n\nclass ArrhythmiaValidator final : public TransformationStrategy {\npublic:\n    std::string schema_id() const override { return \"ecg.v1\"; }\n\n    ValidatedRecord process(const RawRecord& raw) override {\n        auto beats = BeatClassifier::detect_beats(raw.payload);\n        if (!beats.valid()) {\n            throw ValidationError{\"Arrhythmia check failed\"};\n        }\n        ValidatedRecord out;\n        out.patient_id  = raw.patient_id;\n        out.timestamp   = raw.timestamp;\n        out.cleaned_electrodes = beats.cleaned_signal();\n        return out;\n    }\n};\n\nextern \"C\" TransformationStrategy* create_plugin() {\n    return new ArrhythmiaValidator{};\n}\n```\n\n> Compile as:  \n> `g++ -std=c++17 -fPIC -shared ArrhythmiaValidator.cpp -o libarrhythmia_validator.so`\n\n### 9.2. void start();  // non-blocking\n   172→    void stop();   // flush & drain\n   173→\n   174→private:\n   175→    template <typename Stage>\n   176→    void addStage(Stage&& fn) {\n   177→        auto        env.markError(\"Stage failure\");\n   184→                } catch (const std::exception& ex) {\n   185→                    env.markError(ex.what());\n   186→                }\n   187→ 192→    }\n   193→\n   194→    void tracingScope(const EventEnvelope& env);\n   195→\n   196→    std::string name_;\n   197→    std::size_t queueDepth_;\n   198→    tbb::flow::graph g_;\n   199→    std::vector<std::unique_ptr<tbb::flow::graph_node>> nodes_;\n   200→    std::vector<tbb::flow::source_node<EventEnvelope>> sources_;\n   201→};\n   202→\n   203→} // namespace ci360::pipeline\n <tbb/flow_graph.h>\n#include <spdlog/spdlog.h>\n#include <memory>\n#include <functional>\n\nnamespace ci360::pipeline {\n\n/**\n * PipelineScheduler orchestrates a  DAG of ETL stages for a particular\n * signal-family (ECG, BP, SpO2).  It wraps Intel TBB's flow::graph to\n * provide back-pressure, priority scheduling, and runtime instrumentation.\n */\nclass PipelineScheduler {\npublic:\n    using StageFn = std::function<bool(const EventEnvelope&)>;\n\n    explicit PipelineScheduler(std::string_view name, std::size_t queueDepth = 1024);\n\n    template <typename Source, typename... Stages>\n    void build(Source&& src, Stages&&... stages) {\n        // Source node\n        tbb::flow::source_node<EventEnvelope> source_(\n            g_, std::forward<Source>(src), /*is_active=*/false);\n\n        // Fold variadic stages into a linear pipeline\n        (addStage(std::forward<Stages>(stages)), ...);\n\n        // Connect source -> first stage\n        tbb::flow::make_edge(source_, nodes_.front());\n        sources_.push_back(std::move(source_));\n    }\n\n    void start();  // non-blocking\n    void stop();   // flush & drain\n\nprivate:\n    template <typename Stage>\n    void addStage(Stage&& fn) {\n        auto node = std::make_unique<tbb::flow::function_node<EventEnvelope, EventEnvelope>>(\n            g_, queueDepth_,\n            [fn = std::forward<Stage>(fn), this](EventEnvelope env) mutable -> EventEnvelope {\n                tracingScope(env);  // OpenTelemetry instrumentation\n                try {\n                    if (!fn(env))\n                        env.markError(\"Stage failure\");\n                } catch (const std::exception& ex) {\n                    env.markError(ex.what());\n                }\n                return env;\n            });\n        if (!nodes_.empty())\n            tbb::flow::make_edge(*nodes_.back(), *node);\n        nodes_.emplace_back(std::move(node));\n    }\n\n    void tracingScope(const EventEnvelope& env);\n\n    std::string name_;\n    std::size_t queueDepth_;\n    tbb::flow::graph g_;\n    std::vector<std::unique_ptr<tbb::flow::graph_node>> nodes_;\n    std::vector<tbb::flow::source_node<EventEnvelope>> sources_;\n};\n\n} // namespace ci360::pipeline\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6️⃣  Error-Handling & Compensating Flow\n\n```mermaid\nstateDiagram-v2\n    [*] --> HL7/FHIR **Message Ingestion** (real-time)\n    22→2. **ETL Pipeline** for historical replay & batch cohort generation\n    23→3. **Real-Time Alerting** loop\n    24→4. **Dashboard Query** path for clinicians\n    25→\n    26→> NOTE  \n    27→> For brevity, not every error/edge-case branch is shown. HL7 / FHIR Message Ingestion\n    35→\n    36→```mermaid\n    37→sequenceDiagram\n    38→    autonumber\n    39→    participant HIS as Hospital Information System\n    40→    participant IngestSvc as Ingestion Service<br/>(librdkafka Wrapper)\n    41→    participant StreamBus as Event Bus<br/>(Kafka Embedded)\n    42→    participant Validator as Data-Quality<br/>Validator\n    43→    participant Transformer as Transform Service\n    44→    participant DL as Data-Lake Facade\n    45→\n    46→    HIM->>+IngestSvc: HL7/FHIR Message (JSON/ProtoBuf)\n    47→    note over IngestSvc: Parse + envelope\\nwith metadata\n    48→    IngestSvc->>StreamBus: produce(topic=\"hl7.raw\", msg)\n    49→    StreamBus--)IngestSvc: acks=all\n    50→\n    51→    %% Real-time fan-out for quality validation\n    52→    StreamBus->>+Validator: consume(topic=\"hl7.raw\")\n    53→    alt msg.valid?\n    54→        Validator-->>StreamBus: produce(\"hl7.validated\", msg)\n    55→    else validation_failed\n    56→        Validator-->>StreamBus: produce(\"hl7.error\", errorReport)\n    57→        Note over StreamBus,Validator: Error topic triggers<br/>Observer-Pattern hooks\n    58→    end\n    59→    Validator-->>-StreamBus: acks=1\n    60→\n    61→    %% Transformation\n    62→    StreamBus->>+Transformer: consume(\"hl7.validated\")\n    63→    Transformer->>Transformer: StrategyPattern: select policy\n    64→    Transformer-->>StreamBus: produce(\"hl7.transformed\", parquetChunk)\n    65→    Transformer-->>-StreamBus: acks\n    66→\n    67→    %% Durable storage\n    68→    StreamBus->>+DL: ingest(\"hl7.transformed\")\n    69→    DL-->>-StreamBus: commit offsets\n    70→```\n    71→\n    72→Key Points  \n    73→* The ingestion service uses *exactly-once* semantics (`enable.idempotence=true`).  \n    74→* Validation leverages ICU-10 ontologies and arrhythmia rules.  \n    75→* Apache Parquet chunks are staged in write-ahead logs before final commit.\n    76→\n    77→---\n    78→\n    79→## 2. ETL Batch Pipeline (Historical Replay)\n    80→\n    81→```mermaid\n    82→sequenceDiagram\n    83→    autonumber\n    84→    participant Scheduler as Batch Scheduler\n    85→    participant BatchOrch as ETL Orchestrator\n    86→    participant Loader as Data Loader\n    87→    participant TBB as Intel TBB Pool\n    88→    participant DL as Data-Lake Facade\n    89→    participant Curated as Curated Store\n    90→\n    91→    Scheduler->>BatchOrch: cron( \"02:00 UTC\" )\n    92→    BatchOrch->>Loader: replayRange( 24h )\n    93→    Loader->>DL: scanPartitions(date=Y-1)\n    94→    DL-->>Loader: rawChunks[N]\n    95→\n    96→    loop Parallel TBB Tasks (N)\n    97→        Loader->>+TBB: submit(task_i)\n    98→        TBB->>+Loader: transform(chunk_i)\n    99→        TBB->>Curated: writeParquet(chunk_i')\n   100→        Curated-->>-TBB: ACK\n   101→    end\n   102→    Loader-->>BatchOrch: JobReport(metrics)\n   103→    BatchOrch-->Scheduler: success | failure\n   104→```\n   105→\n   106→Highlights  \n   107→* Uses *Pipeline Pattern* with *TBB flow_graph* to maximize I/O and CPU concurrency.  \n   108→* All intermediate artifacts are checksummed (SHA-256) before commit.  \n   109→\n   110→---\n   111→\n   112→## 3. Real-Time Alerting Flow\n   113→\n   114→```mermaid\n   115→sequenceDiagram\n   116→    autonumber\n   117→    participant Wearable as Wearable ECG Device\n   118→    participant IngestSvc as Ingestion Service\n   119→    participant StreamBus\n   120→    participant ArrAlgo as Arrhythmia Algorithm\n   121→    participant AlertSvc as Alert Service\n   122→    participant NurseUI as Nurse Station UI\n   123→\n   124→    Wearable->>IngestSvc: BLE Packet (ECG waveform)\n   125→    IngestSvc->>StreamBus: produce(\"ecg.raw\")\n   126→    StreamBus->>ArrAlgo: consume(\"ecg.raw\")\n   127→    ArrAlgo->>ArrAlgo: FFT + ML Infer\n   128→    alt arrhythmia_detected\n   129→        ArrAlgo-->>StreamBus: produce(\"ecg.alert\", AlertMsg)\n   130→        StreamBus->>AlertSvc: consume(\"ecg.alert\")\n   131→        AlertSvc-->>NurseUI: push-notify(AlertMsg)\n   132→    else normal\n   133→        ArrAlgo-->>StreamBus: produce(\"ecg.normal\")\n   134→    end\n   135→```\n   136→\n   137→Design Decisions  \n   138→* Arrhythmia algorithm is dynamically loaded via `dlopen` enabling hot-swaps.  \n   139→* Alert Service rate-limits duplicates within a *60-second sliding window*.  \n   140→\n   141→---\n   142→\n   143→## 4. Dashboard Ad-Hoc Query\n   144→\n   145→```mermaid\n   146→sequenceDiagram\n   147→    autonumber\n   148→    participant Clinician as Web Browser\n   149→    participant VizSvc as Visualization Service\n   150→    participant Cache as Query Cache (Redis)\n   151→    participant QueryEng as Analytics Engine\n   152→    participant DL as Data-Lake Facade\n   153→\n   154→    Clinician->>VizSvc: HTTP GET /patient/1234?range=8h\n   155→    VizSvc->>Cache: lookup(hash)\n   156→    alt hit\n   157→        Cache-->>VizSvc: parquet + meta\n   158→        VizSvc-->>Clinician: 200 OK (cached data)\n   159→    else miss\n   160→        VizSvc->>QueryEng: plan(SQL++ request)\n   161→        QueryEng->>DL: execute(plan)\n   162→        DL-->>QueryEng: ArrowTable\n   163→        QueryEng->>Cache: store(hash, ArrowTable)\n   164→        VizSvc-->>Clinician: 200 OK (fresh data)\n   165→    end\n   166→```\n   167→\n   168→Operational Notes  \n   169→* Arrow Flight is used for zero-copy transfers between Query Engine and VizSvc.  \n   170→* Dashboard supports HIPAA “break-glass” auditing; every query is logged to an\n   171→  immutable ledger.\n   172→\n   173→---\n   174→\n   175→## Contributing\n   176→\n   177→Sequence diagrams are source-controlled to prevent documentation drift.\n   178→When updating runtime interactions:\n   179→\n   180→1. The diagrams are\n    intentionally kept implementation-agnostic so that they remain\n    accurate even as concrete class names evolve.  All diagrams use\n    GitHub-flavored Mermaid syntax and can be rendered directly in\n    compatible tooling (e.g., GitHub, MkDocs-Material, IntelliJ).\n-->\n\n# CardioInsight360 – Runtime Sequence Diagrams\n\nCardioInsight360 is built as a single deployable binary but internally\norganizes itself around distinct *logical* services.  The following\nsequence diagrams showcase how these services collaborate across the\nthree most business-critical flows:\n\n1. HL7/FHIR **Message Ingestion** (real-time)\n2. **ETL Pipeline** for historical replay & batch cohort generation\n3. **Real-Time Alerting** loop\n4. **Dashboard Query** path for clinicians\n\n> NOTE  \n> For brevity, not every error/edge-case branch is shown.  Refer to the\n> CPP source files under `src/` for low-level details such as timeouts,\n> retry strategies, circuit-breaker thresholds, and transactional\n> semantics.\n\n---\n\n## 1. HL7 / FHIR Message Ingestion\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    participant HIS as Hospital Information System\n    participant IngestSvc as Ingestion Service<br/>(librdkafka Wrapper)\n    participant StreamBus as Event Bus<br/>(Kafka Embedded)\n    participant Validator as Data-Quality<br/>Validator\n    participant Transformer as Transform Service\n    participant DL as Data-Lake Facade\n\n    HIM->>+IngestSvc: HL7/FHIR Message (JSON/ProtoBuf)\n    note over IngestSvc: Parse + envelope\\nwith metadata\n    IngestSvc->>StreamBus: produce(topic=\"hl7.raw\", msg)\n    StreamBus--)IngestSvc: acks=all\n\n    %% Real-time fan-out for quality validation\n    StreamBus->>+Validator: consume(topic=\"hl7.raw\")\n    alt msg.valid?\n        Validator-->>StreamBus: produce(\"hl7.validated\", msg)\n    else validation_failed\n        Validator-->>StreamBus: produce(\"hl7.error\", errorReport)\n        Note over StreamBus,Validator: Error topic triggers<br/>Observer-Pattern hooks\n    end\n    Validator-->>-StreamBus: acks=1\n\n    %% Transformation\n    StreamBus->>+Transformer: consume(\"hl7.validated\")\n    Transformer->>Transformer: StrategyPattern: select policy\n    Transformer-->>StreamBus: produce(\"hl7.transformed\", parquetChunk)\n    Transformer-->>-StreamBus: acks\n\n    %% Durable storage\n    StreamBus->>+DL: ingest(\"hl7.transformed\")\n    DL-->>-StreamBus: commit offsets\n```\n\nKey Points  \n* The ingestion service uses *exactly-once* semantics (`enable.idempotence=true`).  \n* Validation leverages ICU-10 ontologies and arrhythmia rules.  \n* Apache Parquet chunks are staged in write-ahead logs before final commit.\n\n---\n\n## 2. ETL Batch Pipeline (Historical Replay)\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    participant Scheduler as Batch Scheduler\n    participant BatchOrch as ETL Orchestrator\n    participant Loader as Data Loader\n    participant TBB as Intel TBB Pool\n    participant DL as Data-Lake Facade\n    participant Curated as Curated Store\n\n    Scheduler->>BatchOrch: cron( \"02:00 UTC\" )\n    BatchOrch->>Loader: replayRange( 24h )\n    Loader->>DL: scanPartitions(date=Y-1)\n    DL-->>Loader: rawChunks[N]\n\n    loop Parallel TBB Tasks (N)\n        Loader->>+TBB: submit(task_i)\n        TBB->>+Loader: transform(chunk_i)\n        TBB->>Curated: writeParquet(chunk_i')\n        Curated-->>-TBB: ACK\n    end\n    Loader-->>BatchOrch: JobReport(metrics)\n    BatchOrch-->Scheduler: success | failure\n```\n\nHighlights  \n* Uses *Pipeline Pattern* with *TBB flow_graph* to maximize I/O and CPU concurrency.  \n* All intermediate artifacts are checksummed (SHA-256) before commit.  \n\n---\n\n## 3. Real-Time Alerting Flow\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    participant Wearable as Wearable ECG Device\n    participant IngestSvc as Ingestion Service\n    participant StreamBus\n    participant ArrAlgo as Arrhythmia Algorithm\n    participant AlertSvc as Alert Service\n    participant NurseUI as Nurse Station UI\n\n    Wearable->>IngestSvc: BLE Packet (ECG waveform)\n    IngestSvc->>StreamBus: produce(\"ecg.raw\")\n    StreamBus->>ArrAlgo: consume(\"ecg.raw\")\n    ArrAlgo->>ArrAlgo: FFT + ML Infer\n    alt arrhythmia_detected\n        ArrAlgo-->>StreamBus: produce(\"ecg.alert\", AlertMsg)\n        StreamBus->>AlertSvc: consume(\"ecg.alert\")\n        AlertSvc-->>NurseUI: push-notify(AlertMsg)\n    else normal\n        ArrAlgo-->>StreamBus: produce(\"ecg.normal\")\n    end\n```\n\nDesign Decisions  \n* Arrhythmia algorithm is dynamically loaded via `dlopen` enabling hot-swaps.  \n* Alert Service rate-limits duplicates within a *60-second sliding window*.  \n\n---\n\n## 4. Dashboard Ad-Hoc Query\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    participant Clinician as Web Browser\n    participant VizSvc as Visualization Service\n    participant Cache as Query Cache (Redis)\n    participant QueryEng as Analytics Engine\n    participant DL as Data-Lake Facade\n\n    Clinician->>VizSvc: HTTP GET /patient/1234?range=8h\n    VizSvc->>Cache: lookup(hash)\n    alt hit\n        Cache-->>VizSvc: parquet + meta\n        VizSvc-->>Clinician: 200 OK (cached data)\n    else miss\n        VizSvc->>QueryEng: plan(SQL++ request)\n        QueryEng->>DL: execute(plan)\n        DL-->>QueryEng: ArrowTable\n        QueryEng->>Cache: store(hash, ArrowTable)\n        VizSvc-->>Clinician: 200 OK (fresh data)\n    end\n```\n\nOperational Notes  \n* Arrow Flight is used for zero-copy transfers between Query Engine and VizSvc.  \n* Dashboard supports HIPAA “break-glass” auditing; every query is logged to an\n  immutable ledger.\n\n---\n\n## Contributing\n\nSequence diagrams are source-controlled to prevent documentation drift.\nWhen updating runtime interactions:\n\n1. Tag your PR with `docs/sequence-diagram`.\n2. Keep participants *logical*, not concrete class names.\n3. Validate Mermaid syntax locally (`npm i -g @mermaid-js/mermaid-cli`).\n\n```\n","numLines":185,"startLine":1,"totalLines":185}}}

Blocks until all executing tasks complete.\n     */\n    void shutdown()\n    {\n        {\n            std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lk(_stateMtx);\n            if (!_running)\n                return;\n            _running = false;\n        }\n\n        _queue.shutdown();\n        if (_coordinatorThread.joinable())\n            _coordinatorThread.join();\n\n        _taskGroup.wait();\n\n        spdlog::info(\"SchedulingService stopped\");\n        metrics::setGauge(\"scheduler.state\", 0);\n    }\n\n    bool isRunning() const noexcept\n    {\n        std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lk(_stateMtx);\n        return _running;\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    SchedulingService()  = default;\n    ~SchedulingService() { shutdown(); tg.is_idle()       == false &&\n                           outstandingTasks_.load() >= options_.maxConcurrency)\n                    {\n                        std::this_thread::sleep_for(std::chrono::milliseconds{100});\n                    }\n                }\n                else\n                {\n                    std::this_thread::sleep_for(std::chrono::milliseconds{100});\n                }\n            }\n\n            // Wait for in-flight tasks\n            tg.wait();\n const auto nFiles   = batch.files.size();\n            std::size_t success = 0;\n            std::size_t failure = 0;\n\n            observerSafe([&](auto o){ o->onBatchStart(batchId, nFiles); });\n            ci360_log::info(\"[BatchProcessor][\", batchId, \"] Processing \", nFiles, \" files\");\n\n            outstandingTasks_.fetch_add(1);\n\n            auto finishGuard = gsl::finally([&]\n            {\n                observerSafe([&](auto o){ o->onBatchComplete(batchId, success, failure); });\n                outstandingTasks_.fetch_sub(1);\n            });\n\n            for (const auto&        success++;\n                    observerSafe([&](auto o){ o->onFileProcessed(batchId, raw.path); });\n                }\n                catch (const std::exception& ex)\n                {\n                    ci360_log::error(\"[BatchProcessor][\", batchId, \"] Failed to process \",\n                                     raw.path.string(), \" – \", ex.what());\n                    failure++;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        DataFrame transformFile(const RawFile& raw)\n        {\n            auto strategyOpt = registry_->findStrategy(raw.modalityHint);\n            if (!strategyOpt)\n                throw BatchProcessingError(\"Unknown modality: \" + raw.modalityHint);\n\n            const auto& strategy = *strategyOpt;\n            if (!strategy)\n                throw BatchProcessingError(\"Transformation strategy is null for modality: \"\n                                           + raw.modalityHint);\n\n            try\n            {\n                return strategy->transform(raw);\n            }\n            catch (const std::exception& ex)\n            {\n                throw BatchProcessingError(std::string{\"Strategy error: \"} + ex.what());\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Helper that checks observer_ before calling\n        template <typename Fn>\n        void observerSafe(Fn&& fn)\n        {\n            if               {\n                    ci360_log::warn(\"[BatchProcessor] Observer callback failed: \", ex.what());\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n    private:\n                   */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\nnamespace\n{\n\nconstexpr std::size_t kDefaultChunkRows = 65'536; // Row-group size ≈ 64 k rows.\nconstexpr int         kAesKeyBits       = 256;\nconstexpr int         kAesTagBytes      = 16;\nconstexpr int         kAesIvBytes       = 12;\n\n/**\n * A very small RAII wrapper around OpenSSL’s EVP_CIPHER_CTX.\n */\nclass EvpCipherCtx final\n{\npublic:\n    EvpCipherCtx()\n        : ctx_{ EVP_CIPHER_CTX_new() }\n    {\n        if (!ctx_) { throw std::runtime_error(\"EVP_CIPHER_CTX_new failed\"); }\n    }\n    ~EvpCipherCtx() { EVP_CIPHER_CTX_free(ctx_); }\n    EVP_CIPHER_CTX* get() noexcept { return ctx_; }\n\n    EvpCipherCtx(const EvpCipherCtx&)            = delete;\n    EvpCipherCtx& operator=(const EvpCipherCtx&) = delete;\n\nprivate:\n    EVP_CIPHER_CTX* ctx_;\n};\n\n/**\n * Encrypts `src` into `dst` using AES-256-GCM.\n * Layout of dst = [ IV | TAG | CIPHERTEXT ].\n */\nvoid aes256_gcm_encrypt(const std::vector<std::uint8_t>& src,\n                        const std::array<std::uint8_t, 32>& key,\n                        std::vector<std::uint8_t>&        dst)\n{\n    std::array<std::uint8_t, kAesIvBytes> iv{};\n    if (RAND_bytes(iv.data(), iv.size()) != 1) { throw std::runtime_error(\"RAND_bytes failed\"); }\n\n    const EVP_CIPHER* alg = EVP_aes_256_gcm();\n\n    EvpCipherCtx ctx;\n    if (EVP_EncryptInit_ex(ctx.get(), alg, nullptr, nullptr, nullptr) != 1)\n        throw std::runtime_error(\"EVP_EncryptInit_ex failed (alg)\");\n\n    if (EVP_CIPHER_CTX_ctrl(ctx.get(), EVP_CTRL_GCM_SET_IVLEN, iv.size(), nullptr) != 1)\n        throw std::runtime_error(\"EVP_CIPHER_CTX_ctrl failed (iv len)\");\n\n    if (EVP_EncryptInit_ex(ctx.get(), nullptr, nullptr, key.data(), iv.data()) != 1)\n        throw std::runtime_error(\"EVP_EncryptInit_ex failed (key/iv)\");\n\n    std::vector<std::uint8_t> cipher(src.size() + EVP_CIPHER_block_size(alg));\n    int                      out_len = 0;\n\n    if (EVP_EncryptUpdate(ctx.get(), cipher.data(), &out_len, src.data(),\n kAesTagBytes,\n                            const_cast<std::uint8_t*>(tag)) != 1)\n        throw std::runtime_error(\"EVP_Decrypt ctrl (set tag) failed\");\n\n    int tmp_len = 0;\n    if                                const fs::path&                     file_path) const\n{\n    if (opts_.encryption_enabled)\n    {\n        // First write into a buffer, encrypt, then flush to disk\n });\n        ::signal(SIGTERM, [](int) { instance_flag().store(false); });\n\n        // Link flag into the application\n        app.running_.store(true);\n        app.linked_flag_ = &instance_flag();\n    }\n\nprivate:\n    static std::atomic_bool& instance_flag() {\n        static std::atomic_bool flag{true};\n        return flag;\n    }\n\n    std::shared_ptr<Config>        config_;\n    std::unique_ptr<EventBus>      event_bus_;\n    std::unique_ptr<DataLake>      data_lake_;\n    std::unique_ptr<MetricsHub>    metrics_hub_;\n    std::unique_ptr<ETLPipeline>   etl_;\n    std::unique_ptr<SchedulerService> scheduler_;\n\n    std::atomic_bool*              linked_flag_{nullptr};\n    std::atomic_bool               running_{false};\n};\n\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Main\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nint main(int argc, char* argv[]) try {\n    auto cfg_path = argc > 1 ? argv[1] : \"config.json\";\n    if (!std::filesystem::exists(cfg_path)) {\n        std::cerr << \"Config file not found: \" << cfg_path << '\\n';\n        return EXIT_FAILURE;\n    }\n\n    Application app{cfg_path};\n    Application::install_signal_handlers(app);\n\n    app.init();\n    app.run();\n    app.stop();\n\n    return EXIT_SUCCESS;\n} catch (const std::exception& ex) {\n    spdlog::critical(\"Fatal error: {}\", ex.what());\n    return EXIT_FAILURE;\n} catch (...) {\n    spdlog::critical(\"Unknown fatal error\");\n    return EXIT_FAILURE;\n}\n```\n","numLines":413,"startLine":1,"totalLines":413}}}

The analysis traces both the real-time alerting path and batch processing path, identifies the precise resource contention points, and proposes a targeted, minimally invasive solution based on dedicated, prioritized thread pools.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Architectural Trace\n\n### 1.1 Real-Time Alerting Path\n\nThe real-time alerting workload follows this component path from HL7 data ingestion to alert dispatch:\n\n```\nHL7 Source → HL7MllpConnector → Kafka (hl7_raw topic) → IngestionService\n→ EventBusFacade → StreamProcessor → AlertingService → Nurse Station UI\n```\n\n**Detailed Component Flow:**\n\n| Step | Component | File | Description |\n|------|-----------|------|-------------|\n| 1 | **HL7MllpConnector** | `src/ingestion/connectors/hl7_mllp_connector.cpp:201-217` | TCP listener accepting HL7 v2 MLLP messages on dedicated background thread. Parses messages and publishes to Kafka. |\n| 2 | **IngestionService** | `src/ingestion/ingestion_service.cpp:144-196` | Consumes from Kafka topic, validates via HL7Parser/HL7Validator, persists to DataLake, and publishes to internal EventBus. Uses **TBB task_arena** for processing (`arena_.execute(...)` at line 182). |\n| 3 | **EventBusFacade** | `src/event_bus/event_bus_facade.cpp:292-311` | Manages Kafka pub/sub. Launches consumer threads per subscribed topic (`_consumerThreads`). |\n| 4 | **StreamProcessor** | `src/processing/stream_processor.cpp:221-378` | Consumes validated signals, applies transformation strategies (ECG/BP), produces to output topic. Uses **TBB task_group** for async transformations (`taskGroup_.run(...)` at line 308). |\n| 5 | **AlertingService** | `src/services/alerting_service.cpp:197-225` | Consumes from signal topics, evaluates threshold rules, emits alerts to Kafka and notifies UI via WebSocket push. Runs its own worker thread (`worker_` at line 129). |\n\n**Critical Insight:** The StreamProcessor (line 175: `tbb::task_group taskGroup_`) and the IngestionService both use **TBB (Intel Threading Building Blocks)** for parallelization. TBB uses a **global work-stealing thread pool** by default, meaning all TBB-based workloads compete for the same pool of worker threads.\n\n### 1.2 Batch Processing Path\n\nThe batch processing workload follows this component path:\n\n```\nCron Trigger → SchedulingService → BatchProcessor → ETLPipeline\n→ TBB Flow Graph → DataLakeFacade → Parquet Files\n```\n\n**Detailed Component Flow:**\n\n| Step | Component | File | Description |\n|------|-----------|------|-------------|\n| 1 | **SchedulingService** | `src/services/scheduling_service.cpp:219-243,372-382` | Cron-like scheduler using min-heap for task scheduling. Dispatches tasks to **TBB task_group** (`_taskGroup` at line 353). Registers `etl.daily.batch` task at line 377. |\n| 2 | **BatchProcessor** | `src/processing/batch_processor.cpp:164-339` | Manages batch job queue. Worker loop dispatches to **TBB task_group** (`tg.run(...)` at line 229). Processes files using `TransformationRegistry`. |\n| 3 | **ETLPipeline** | `src/processing/etl_pipeline.cpp:361-467` | Core ETL orchestrator. Uses **TBB flow_graph** (`tbb::flow::graph g_` at line 400) with `transformer_concurrency` set to `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` (line 174). Heavy CPU operations: Extract → Transform → Validate → Load. |\n| 4 | **DataLakeFacade** | `src/storage/data_lake_facade.cpp:229-284` | Manages Parquet file I/O. Uses **TBB parallel_for** (`tbb::parallel_for` at line 270) and dedicated **TBB task_arena** (`arena_` at line 231) for parallel chunk writing. |\n\n**Critical Insight:** The ETLPipeline uses `cfg_.transformer_concurrency` which defaults to `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` (all available CPU cores). The DataLakeFacade also uses its own TBB task_arena but the ETLPipeline's flow_graph uses the **global TBB scheduler** with no explicit arena isolation.\n\n### 1.3 Intersection Point: Service Manager\n\nThe ServiceManager (`src/services/service_manager.cpp:220-376`) is responsible for lifecycle management of all services. Key observations:\n\n1. **No Resource Partitioning** (lines 252-276): The `startAll()` method launches all services concurrently using `std::async` but does **not** assign services to specific thread pools or resource partitions.\n\n2. **Equal Treatment** (lines 260-274): All services are started via `kv.second->start()` without any priority differentiation.\n\n3. **No Configuration for Prioritization**: The current configuration (`config/main_config.json.template`) has an `etl.pipeline.max_concurrency` setting (line 97) but this is set to `\"auto\"` and there's no equivalent configuration for real-time workloads or explicit pool segregation.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Root Cause Hypothesis\n\n### 2.1 Primary Contention: CPU and TBB Global Thread Pool\n\nThe root cause of the alerting latency degradation is **CPU resource contention** via the **shared TBB global thread pool**.\n\n**Evidence:**\n\n1. **Shared TBB Scheduler**: Multiple components use TBB without explicit arena isolation:\n   - `StreamProcessor::taskGroup_` (stream_processor.cpp:175)\n   - `SchedulingService::_taskGroup` (scheduling_service.cpp:353)\n   - `BatchProcessor::tg` (batch_processor.cpp:220)\n   - `ETLPipeline` flow_graph (etl_pipeline.cpp:400)\n\n2. **Unbounded Batch Concurrency**: The ETLPipeline defaults to `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` for transformer concurrency (etl_pipeline.cpp:174), meaning it will attempt to use **all available CPU cores**.\n\n3. **TBB Work-Stealing Semantics**: When the ETL batch job runs, its compute-intensive tasks (transformation, validation, Parquet encoding) flood the global TBB work queue. The work-stealing scheduler then prioritizes these tasks equally with real-time StreamProcessor tasks, causing the latency-sensitive alerting path to wait for CPU cycles.\n\n4. **No Priority Mechanism**: The ServiceManager (service_manager.cpp:252-276) starts all services without establishing any resource boundaries or priorities.\n\n### 2.2 Secondary Contention: I/O Pressure\n\nThe DataLakeFacade's parallel Parquet writes (data_lake_facade.cpp:269-283) create I/O contention that can indirectly affect the EventBusFacade's Kafka operations if:\n- The Kafka broker uses the same disk subsystem\n- System-level I/O schedulers don't differentiate between workloads\n\nHowever, this is a **secondary** issue. The primary bottleneck is CPU contention.\n\n### 2.3 Why Not Event Bus Contention?\n\nWhile the EventBusFacade could theoretically be a bottleneck, the analysis shows:\n- Real-time and batch use **different Kafka topics** (ingest vs. transform topics)\n- The embedded Kafka broker handles topic isolation\n- The alerting path uses dedicated consumer threads per topic (event_bus_facade.cpp:299-308)\n\n---\n\n## 3. Solution Proposal\n\n### 3.1 Conceptual Change: Workload-Aware Thread Pool Partitioning\n\nIntroduce **dedicated, prioritized TBB task_arenas** for different workload categories:\n\n| Pool Name | Priority | Concurrency | Assigned Components |\n|-----------|----------|-------------|---------------------|\n| `realtime_pool` | High | 25-33% of cores (min 2) | StreamProcessor, AlertingService consumers |\n| `batch_pool` | Normal | 50-66% of cores | ETLPipeline, BatchProcessor |\n| `ingestion_pool` | High | 10-25% of cores (min 2) | IngestionService |\n\nThis ensures the real-time path always has dedicated CPU resources that cannot be starved by batch workloads.\n\n### 3.2 Files Requiring Modification\n\n#### 3.2.1 `config/main_config.json.template`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\nAdd a new `thread_pools` configuration section:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"thread_pools\": {\n    \"realtime\": {\n      \"concurrency\": \"auto:25%\",\n      \"min_threads\": 2,\n      \"priority\": \"high\"\n    },\n    \"batch\": {\n      \"concurrency\": \"auto:50%\",\n      \"min_threads\": 4,\n      \"priority\": \"normal\"\n    },\n    \"ingestion\": {\n      \"concurrency\": \"auto:15%\",\n      \"min_threads\": 2,\n      \"priority\": \"high\"\n    }\n  },\n  \"workload_assignment\": {\n    \"StreamProcessor\": \"realtime\",\n    \"AlertingService\": \"realtime\",\n    \"IngestionService\": \"ingestion\",\n    \"ETLPipeline\": \"batch\",\n    \"BatchProcessor\": \"batch\",\n    \"SchedulingService\": \"batch\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### 3.2.2 `src/core/config_manager.cpp`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\n1. Add parsing logic for the new `thread_pools` section (around line 125)\n2. Add accessor methods for thread pool configuration\n3. Validate that percentages sum to ≤100% and min_threads are achievable\n\n**High-Level Changes:**\n\n```cpp\n// Add to ConfigManager class:\nstruct ThreadPoolConfig {\n    std::string name;\n    int concurrency;        // Absolute or percentage\n    int min_threads;\n    std::string priority;   // \"high\", \"normal\", \"low\"\n};\n\nstd::vector<ThreadPoolConfig> getThreadPoolConfigs() const;\nstd::optional<std::string> getWorkloadPoolAssignment(const std::string& component) const;\n```\n\n#### 3.2.3 `src/services/service_manager.cpp` (Primary Changes)\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\nThis is the central file requiring modification. The ServiceManager must:\n\n1. **Create and own named TBB task_arenas** (new member variables around line 374)\n2. **Provide pool accessors** for services to obtain their assigned arena\n3. **Configure arenas** based on `thread_pools` configuration during construction\n\n**High-Level Implementation:**\n\n```cpp\n// Add to ServiceManager class (around line 362-375):\nprivate:\n    std::unordered_map<std::string, std::unique_ptr<tbb::task_arena>> _threadPools;\n    std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string> _componentPoolMap;\n\npublic:\n    // Called during ServiceManager initialization\n    void initializeThreadPools(const core::ConfigManager& config) {\n        auto poolConfigs = config.getThreadPoolConfigs();\n\n        for (const auto& cfg : poolConfigs) {\n            int threads = calculateThreadCount(cfg);\n            auto arena = std::make_unique<tbb::task_arena>(threads);\n            _threadPools.emplace(cfg.name, std::move(arena));\n        }\n\n        // Load component-to-pool mapping\n        _componentPoolMap = config.getWorkloadAssignments();\n    }\n\n    // Services call this to get their assigned arena\n    tbb::task_arena& getPoolForComponent(const std::string& componentName) {\n        auto it = _componentPoolMap.find(componentName);\n        if (it != _componentPoolMap.end()) {\n            auto poolIt = _threadPools.find(it->second);\n            if (poolIt != _threadPools.end()) {\n                return *poolIt->second;\n            }\n        }\n        // Fallback to default arena\n        static tbb::task_arena defaultArena;\n        return defaultArena;\n    }\n```\n\n**Modification to `startAll()` (lines 252-276):**\n\n```cpp\nvoid startAll() {\n    std::shared_lock<std::shared_mutex> readLock(_rwMtx);\n    spdlog::info(\"Starting {} services with workload-aware thread pools...\", _services.size());\n\n    // Start services, passing their assigned thread pool\n    std::vector<std::future<void>> futures;\n    futures.reserve(_services.size());\n\n    for (auto& kv : _services) {\n        auto& arena = getPoolForComponent(kv.first);\n        futures.emplace_back(std::async(std::launch::async, [&kv, &arena] {\n            try {\n                // Services that support arena injection will use it\n                kv.second->start(&arena);  // Modified interface\n            } catch (const std::exception& ex) {\n                spdlog::error(\"Failed to start [{}]: {}\", kv.first, ex.what());\n            }\n        }));\n    }\n\n    for (auto& f : futures) f.wait();\n    spdlog::info(\"All services started with workload isolation\");\n}\n```\n\n#### 3.2.4 `src/services/service_manager.h`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\n1.  // NEW\n};\n```\n\n2. **workerLoop modification** (around line 218-251):\n```cpp\nvoid workerLoop() {\n    tbb::task_group tg;\n    auto execute = [this](auto fn) {\n        if (options_.arena) {\n            options_.arena->execute(fn);\n        } else {\n            fn();\n        }\n    };\n\n    while (!stopped_.load() || !requestQueue_.empty()) {\n        BatchRequest req;\n        if (requestQueue_.try_pop(req)) {\n            auto self = shared_from_this();\n            execute([&tg, this, self, r = std::move(req)]() mutable {\n                tg.run([this, self, r = std::move(r)]() mutable {\n                    processBatch(std::move(r));\n                });\n            });\n            // ... throttling logic ...\n        }\n    }\n\n    execute([&tg] { tg.wait(); });\n}\n```\n\n#### 3.2.9 `src/services/scheduling_service.cpp`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\n1. However, if alert rule evaluation becomes CPU-intensive (e.g., complex ML models), it should be modified to use the realtime pool:\n\n1. Add optional arena parameter to constructor (around line 169-188)\n2. Use arena for any parallel rule evaluation\n\n---\n\n## 4. Resolution Mechanism\n\n### 4.1 How This Solves the Problem\n\n1. **Resource Isolation**: By creating separate `tbb::task_arena` instances for real-time and batch workloads, we ensure that:\n   - Real-time workloads (StreamProcessor, AlertingService) have **guaranteed access** to a minimum number of dedicated threads\n   - Batch workloads (ETLPipeline, BatchProcessor) cannot consume threads allocated to the real-time pool\n\n2. **Priority Enforcement**: TBB's task_arena with limited concurrency acts as a \"soft partition\" - tasks submitted to the real-time arena will not be work-stolen by batch arena threads.\n\n3. **Graceful Degradation**: If real-time workload exceeds its allocation, it will slow down gracefully. The batch pool cannot cause starvation because it operates on a completely separate thread budget.\n\n4. **Configuration Flexibility**: The solution allows operators to tune pool sizes based on their specific deployment (e.g., more cores for batch on overnight runs, more for real-time during clinic hours).\n\n### 4.2 Impact Analysis\n\n| Component | Change Impact | Testing Required |\n|-----------|---------------|------------------|\n| ConfigManager | Low - additive changes | Unit tests for new config parsing |\n| ServiceManager | Medium - central orchestration changes | Integration tests for service startup |\n| StreamProcessor | Medium - constructor change | Performance benchmarks, unit tests |\n| ETLPipeline | Medium - arena injection | Batch job tests, performance benchmarks |\n| BatchProcessor | Medium - arena injection | Batch job tests |\n| SchedulingService | Low - arena injection | Task scheduling tests |\n\n### 4.3 Backward Compatibility\n\nThe solution maintains backward compatibility:\n- All new configuration is **optional** with sensible defaults\n- If `thread_pools` config is absent, the system falls back to current behavior\n- Arena injection parameters are optional (nullptr = use global scheduler)\n\n---\n\n## 5. Summary\n\n### 5.1 Root Cause\nThe alerting latency issue is caused by **CPU resource contention** between real-time and batch workloads via the **shared TBB global thread pool**. The ETLPipeline's heavy transformations flood the global work queue, starving the latency-sensitive StreamProcessor and AlertingService of CPU cycles.\n\n### 5.2 Solution\nIntroduce **workload-aware thread pool partitioning** using TBB task_arenas:\n- Create named thread pools with configurable sizes\n- Assign services to pools based on latency requirements\n- Modify key components to accept injected arenas\n\n### 5.3 Primary Files Modified\n\n| File | Type of Change |\n|------|----------------|\n| `config/main_config.json.template` | Configuration schema extension |\n| `src/core/config_manager.cpp/hpp` | Config parsing for thread pools |\n| `src/services/service_manager.cpp/h` | Pool creation and management |\n| `src/processing/stream_processor.cpp/h` | Arena {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01R3KVb6XNqTm3eTMLPGRDLA","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /logs/agent/solution.md"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4ff65527-13e4-4260-b593-543ac3a5af5d","uuid":"75b039f6-816f-46cc-9fb0-cd97e451f1f2","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/logs/agent/solution.md","content":"# CardioInsight360 – Alerting Latency Diagnosis and Resolution Plan\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nThis document provides a comprehensive architectural analysis of the CardioInsight360 system to diagnose the root cause of real-time alerting latency degradation (300-500% increase) observed when batch ETL jobs are running. The analysis traces both the real-time alerting path and batch processing path, identifies the precise resource contention points, and proposes a targeted, minimally invasive solution based on dedicated, prioritized thread pools.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Architectural Trace\n\n### 1.1 Real-Time Alerting Path\n\nThe real-time alerting workload follows this component path from HL7 data ingestion to alert dispatch:\n\n```\nHL7 Source → HL7MllpConnector → Kafka (hl7_raw topic) → IngestionService\n→ EventBusFacade → StreamProcessor → AlertingService → Nurse Station UI\n```\n\n**Detailed Component Flow:**\n\n| Step | Component | File | Description |\n|------|-----------|------|-------------|\n| 1 | **HL7MllpConnector** | `src/ingestion/connectors/hl7_mllp_connector.cpp:201-217` | TCP listener accepting HL7 v2 MLLP messages on dedicated background thread. Parses messages and publishes to Kafka. |\n| 2 | **IngestionService** | `src/ingestion/ingestion_service.cpp:144-196` | Consumes from Kafka topic, validates via HL7Parser/HL7Validator, persists to DataLake, and publishes to internal EventBus. Uses **TBB task_arena** for processing (`arena_.execute(...)` at line 182). |\n| 3 | **EventBusFacade** | `src/event_bus/event_bus_facade.cpp:292-311` | Manages Kafka pub/sub. Launches consumer threads per subscribed topic (`_consumerThreads`). |\n| 4 | **StreamProcessor** | `src/processing/stream_processor.cpp:221-378` | Consumes validated signals, applies transformation strategies (ECG/BP), produces to output topic. Uses **TBB task_group** for async transformations (`taskGroup_.run(...)` at line 308). |\n| 5 | **AlertingService** | `src/services/alerting_service.cpp:197-225` | Consumes from signal topics, evaluates threshold rules, emits alerts to Kafka and notifies UI via WebSocket push. Runs its own worker thread (`worker_` at line 129). |\n\n**Critical Insight:** The StreamProcessor (line 175: `tbb::task_group taskGroup_`) and the IngestionService both use **TBB (Intel Threading Building Blocks)** for parallelization. TBB uses a **global work-stealing thread pool** by default, meaning all TBB-based workloads compete for the same pool of worker threads.\n\n### 1.2 Batch Processing Path\n\nThe batch processing workload follows this component path:\n\n```\nCron Trigger → SchedulingService → BatchProcessor → ETLPipeline\n→ TBB Flow Graph → DataLakeFacade → Parquet Files\n```\n\n**Detailed Component Flow:**\n\n| Step | Component | File | Description |\n|------|-----------|------|-------------|\n| 1 | **SchedulingService** | `src/services/scheduling_service.cpp:219-243,372-382` | Cron-like scheduler using min-heap for task scheduling. Dispatches tasks to **TBB task_group** (`_taskGroup` at line 353). Registers `etl.daily.batch` task at line 377. |\n| 2 | **BatchProcessor** | `src/processing/batch_processor.cpp:164-339` | Manages batch job queue. Worker loop dispatches to **TBB task_group** (`tg.run(...)` at line 229). Processes files using `TransformationRegistry`. |\n| 3 | **ETLPipeline** | `src/processing/etl_pipeline.cpp:361-467` | Core ETL orchestrator. Uses **TBB flow_graph** (`tbb::flow::graph g_` at line 400) with `transformer_concurrency` set to `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` (line 174). Heavy CPU operations: Extract → Transform → Validate → Load. |\n| 4 | **DataLakeFacade** | `src/storage/data_lake_facade.cpp:229-284` | Manages Parquet file I/O. Uses **TBB parallel_for** (`tbb::parallel_for` at line 270) and dedicated **TBB task_arena** (`arena_` at line 231) for parallel chunk writing. |\n\n**Critical Insight:** The ETLPipeline uses `cfg_.transformer_concurrency` which defaults to `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` (all available CPU cores). The DataLakeFacade also uses its own TBB task_arena but the ETLPipeline's flow_graph uses the **global TBB scheduler** with no explicit arena isolation.\n\n### 1.3 Intersection Point: Service Manager\n\nThe ServiceManager (`src/services/service_manager.cpp:220-376`) is responsible for lifecycle management of all services. Key observations:\n\n1. **No Resource Partitioning** (lines 252-276): The `startAll()` method launches all services concurrently using `std::async` but does **not** assign services to specific thread pools or resource partitions.\n\n2. **Equal Treatment** (lines 260-274): All services are started via `kv.second->start()` without any priority differentiation.\n\n3. **No Configuration for Prioritization**: The current configuration (`config/main_config.json.template`) has an `etl.pipeline.max_concurrency` setting (line 97) but this is set to `\"auto\"` and there's no equivalent configuration for real-time workloads or explicit pool segregation.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Root Cause Hypothesis\n\n### 2.1 Primary Contention: CPU and TBB Global Thread Pool\n\nThe root cause of the alerting latency degradation is **CPU resource contention** via the **shared TBB global thread pool**.\n\n**Evidence:**\n\n1. **Shared TBB Scheduler**: Multiple components use TBB without explicit arena isolation:\n   - `StreamProcessor::taskGroup_` (stream_processor.cpp:175)\n   - `SchedulingService::_taskGroup` (scheduling_service.cpp:353)\n   - `BatchProcessor::tg` (batch_processor.cpp:220)\n   - `ETLPipeline` flow_graph (etl_pipeline.cpp:400)\n\n2. **Unbounded Batch Concurrency**: The ETLPipeline defaults to `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` for transformer concurrency (etl_pipeline.cpp:174), meaning it will attempt to use **all available CPU cores**.\n\n3. **TBB Work-Stealing Semantics**: When the ETL batch job runs, its compute-intensive tasks (transformation, validation, Parquet encoding) flood the global TBB work queue. The work-stealing scheduler then prioritizes these tasks equally with real-time StreamProcessor tasks, causing the latency-sensitive alerting path to wait for CPU cycles.\n\n4. **No Priority Mechanism**: The ServiceManager (service_manager.cpp:252-276) starts all services without establishing any resource boundaries or priorities.\n\n### 2.2 Secondary Contention: I/O Pressure\n\nThe DataLakeFacade's parallel Parquet writes (data_lake_facade.cpp:269-283) create I/O contention that can indirectly affect the EventBusFacade's Kafka operations if:\n- The Kafka broker uses the same disk subsystem\n- System-level I/O schedulers don't differentiate between workloads\n\nHowever, this is a **secondary** issue. The primary bottleneck is CPU contention.\n\n### 2.3 Why Not Event Bus Contention?\n\nWhile the EventBusFacade could theoretically be a bottleneck, the analysis shows:\n- Real-time and batch use **different Kafka topics** (ingest vs. transform topics)\n- The embedded Kafka broker handles topic isolation\n- The alerting path uses dedicated consumer threads per topic (event_bus_facade.cpp:299-308)\n\n---\n\n## 3. Solution Proposal\n\n### 3.1 Conceptual Change: Workload-Aware Thread Pool Partitioning\n\nIntroduce **dedicated, prioritized TBB task_arenas** for different workload categories:\n\n| Pool Name | Priority | Concurrency | Assigned Components |\n|-----------|----------|-------------|---------------------|\n| `realtime_pool` | High | 25-33% of cores (min 2) | StreamProcessor, AlertingService consumers |\n| `batch_pool` | Normal | 50-66% of cores | ETLPipeline, BatchProcessor |\n| `ingestion_pool` | High | 10-25% of cores (min 2) | IngestionService |\n\nThis ensures the real-time path always has dedicated CPU resources that cannot be starved by batch workloads.\n\n### 3.2 Files Requiring Modification\n\n#### 3.2.1 `config/main_config.json.template`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\nAdd a new `thread_pools` configuration section:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"thread_pools\": {\n    \"realtime\": {\n      \"concurrency\": \"auto:25%\",\n      \"min_threads\": 2,\n      \"priority\": \"high\"\n    },\n    \"batch\": {\n      \"concurrency\": \"auto:50%\",\n      \"min_threads\": 4,\n      \"priority\": \"normal\"\n    },\n    \"ingestion\": {\n      \"concurrency\": \"auto:15%\",\n      \"min_threads\": 2,\n      \"priority\": \"high\"\n    }\n  },\n  \"workload_assignment\": {\n    \"StreamProcessor\": \"realtime\",\n    \"AlertingService\": \"realtime\",\n    \"IngestionService\": \"ingestion\",\n    \"ETLPipeline\": \"batch\",\n    \"BatchProcessor\": \"batch\",\n    \"SchedulingService\": \"batch\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### 3.2.2 `src/core/config_manager.cpp`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\n1. Add parsing logic for the new `thread_pools` section (around line 125)\n2. Add accessor methods for thread pool configuration\n3. Validate that percentages sum to ≤100% and min_threads are achievable\n\n**High-Level Changes:**\n\n```cpp\n// Add to ConfigManager class:\nstruct ThreadPoolConfig {\n    std::string name;\n    int concurrency;        // Absolute or percentage\n    int min_threads;\n    std::string priority;   // \"high\", \"normal\", \"low\"\n};\n\nstd::vector<ThreadPoolConfig> getThreadPoolConfigs() const;\nstd::optional<std::string> getWorkloadPoolAssignment(const std::string& component) const;\n```\n\n#### 3.2.3 `src/services/service_manager.cpp` (Primary Changes)\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\nThis is the central file requiring modification. The ServiceManager must:\n\n1. **Create and own named TBB task_arenas** (new member variables around line 374)\n2. **Provide pool accessors** for services to obtain their assigned arena\n3. **Configure arenas** based on `thread_pools` configuration during construction\n\n**High-Level Implementation:**\n\n```cpp\n// Add to ServiceManager class (around line 362-375):\nprivate:\n    std::unordered_map<std::string, std::unique_ptr<tbb::task_arena>> _threadPools;\n    std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string> _componentPoolMap;\n\npublic:\n    // Called during ServiceManager initialization\n    void initializeThreadPools(const core::ConfigManager& config) {\n        auto poolConfigs = config.getThreadPoolConfigs();\n\n        for (const auto& cfg : poolConfigs) {\n            int threads = calculateThreadCount(cfg);\n            auto arena = std::make_unique<tbb::task_arena>(threads);\n            _threadPools.emplace(cfg.name, std::move(arena));\n        }\n\n        // Load component-to-pool mapping\n        _componentPoolMap = config.getWorkloadAssignments();\n    }\n\n    // Services call this to get their assigned arena\n    tbb::task_arena& getPoolForComponent(const std::string& componentName) {\n        auto it = _componentPoolMap.find(componentName);\n        if (it != _componentPoolMap.end()) {\n            auto poolIt = _threadPools.find(it->second);\n            if (poolIt != _threadPools.end()) {\n                return *poolIt->second;\n            }\n        }\n        // Fallback to default arena\n        static tbb::task_arena defaultArena;\n        return defaultArena;\n    }\n```\n\n**Modification to `startAll()` (lines 252-276):**\n\n```cpp\nvoid startAll() {\n    std::shared_lock<std::shared_mutex> readLock(_rwMtx);\n    spdlog::info(\"Starting {} services with workload-aware thread pools...\", _services.size());\n\n    // Start services, passing their assigned thread pool\n    std::vector<std::future<void>> futures;\n    futures.reserve(_services.size());\n\n    for (auto& kv : _services) {\n        auto& arena = getPoolForComponent(kv.first);\n        futures.emplace_back(std::async(std::launch::async, [&kv, &arena] {\n            try {\n                // Services that support arena injection will use it\n                kv.second->start(&arena);  // Modified interface\n            } catch (const std::exception& ex) {\n                spdlog::error(\"Failed to start [{}]: {}\", kv.first, ex.what());\n            }\n        }));\n    }\n\n    for (auto& f : futures) f.wait();\n    spdlog::info(\"All services started with workload isolation\");\n}\n```\n\n#### 3.2.4 `src/services/service_manager.h`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\n1. Add `#include <tbb/task_arena.h>`\n2. Add method declarations for `initializeThreadPools()` and `getPoolForComponent()`\n3. Add private member variables for pools and mappings\n\n#### 3.2.5 `src/processing/stream_processor.cpp`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\nModify `StreamProcessor` to accept and use an external `tbb::task_arena`:\n\n1. **Constructor modification** (around line 221):\n```cpp\nStreamProcessor::StreamProcessor(KafkaConfig config,\n                                 std::size_t parallelism,\n                                 tbb::task_arena* arena)  // NEW parameter\n    : cfg_{std::move(config)}\n    , maxInFlight_{parallelism == 0 ? 1 : parallelism}\n    , externalArena_{arena}  // NEW member\n{\n```\n\n2. **asyncTransform modification** (around line 305-363):\n```cpp\nvoid StreamProcessor::asyncTransform(const std::string& payload,\n                                     const std::optional<std::string> key)\n{\n    auto transform_task = [this, payload, key] {\n        // ... existing transformation logic ...\n    };\n\n    // Execute in assigned arena if provided\n    if (externalArena_) {\n        externalArena_->execute([this, task = std::move(transform_task)] {\n            taskGroup_.run(task);\n        });\n    } else {\n        taskGroup_.run(transform_task);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n3. **Add member variable** (around line 175):\n```cpp\ntbb::task_arena* externalArena_{nullptr};  // Optional: externally managed arena\n```\n\n#### 3.2.6 `src/processing/stream_processor.h`\n\n**Changes Required:**\n\n1. However, if alert rule evaluation becomes CPU-intensive (e.g., complex ML models), it should be modified to use the realtime pool:\n\n1. Add optional arena parameter to constructor (around line 169-188)\n2. Use arena for any parallel rule evaluation\n\n---\n\n## 4. Resolution Mechanism\n\n### 4.1 How This Solves the Problem\n\n1. **Resource Isolation**: By creating separate `tbb::task_arena` instances for real-time and batch workloads, we ensure that:\n   - Real-time workloads (StreamProcessor, AlertingService) have **guaranteed access** to a minimum number of dedicated threads\n   - Batch workloads (ETLPipeline, BatchProcessor) cannot consume threads allocated to the real-time pool\n\n2. **Priority Enforcement**: TBB's task_arena with limited concurrency acts as a \"soft partition\" - tasks submitted to the real-time arena will not be work-stolen by batch arena threads.\n\n3. **Graceful Degradation**: If real-time workload exceeds its allocation, it will slow down gracefully. The batch pool cannot cause starvation because it operates on a completely separate thread budget.\n\n4. **Configuration Flexibility**: The solution allows operators to tune pool sizes based on their specific deployment (e.g., more cores for batch on overnight runs, more for real-time during clinic hours).\n\n### 4.2 Impact Analysis\n\n| Component | Change Impact | Testing Required |\n|-----------|---------------|------------------|\n| ConfigManager | Low - additive changes | Unit tests for new config parsing |\n| ServiceManager | Medium - central orchestration changes | Integration tests for service startup |\n| StreamProcessor | Medium - constructor change | Performance benchmarks, unit tests |\n| ETLPipeline | Medium - arena injection | Batch job tests, performance benchmarks |\n| BatchProcessor | Medium - arena injection | Batch job tests |\n| SchedulingService | Low - arena injection | Task scheduling tests |\n\n### 4.3 Backward Compatibility\n\nThe solution maintains backward compatibility:\n- All new configuration is **optional** with sensible defaults\n- If `thread_pools` config is absent, the system falls back to current behavior\n- Arena injection parameters are optional (nullptr = use global scheduler)\n\n---\n\n## 5. Summary\n\n### 5.1 Root Cause\nThe alerting latency issue is caused by **CPU resource contention** between real-time and batch workloads via the **shared TBB global thread pool**. The ETLPipeline's heavy transformations flood the global work queue, starving the latency-sensitive StreamProcessor and AlertingService of CPU cycles.\n\n### 5.2 Solution\nIntroduce **workload-aware thread pool partitioning** using TBB task_arenas:\n- Create named thread pools with configurable sizes\n- Assign services to pools based on latency requirements\n- Modify key components to accept injected arenas\n\n### 5.3 Primary Files Modified\n\n| File | Type of Change |\n|------|----------------|\n| `config/main_config.json.template` | Configuration schema extension |\n| `src/core/config_manager.cpp/hpp` | Config parsing for thread pools |\n| `src/services/service_manager.cpp/h` | Pool creation and management |\n| `src/processing/stream_processor.cpp/h` | Arena injection |\n| `src/processing/etl_pipeline.cpp` | Arena injection |\n| `src/processing/batch_processor.cpp` | Arena injection |\n| `src/services/scheduling_service.cpp` | Arena injection |\n\n### 5.4 Expected Outcome\nWith this solution implemented:\n- Real-time alerting latency will remain **stable** regardless of batch workload\n- Batch jobs will use remaining CPU capacity without impacting SLAs\n- The solution is **minimally invasive**, extending rather than replacing existing patterns\n- Configuration is **tunable** for different deployment scenarios\n\n---\n\n## Appendix A: Here's a summary of my findings:\n\n## Summary\n\n### Root Cause Diagnosis\nThe real-time alerting latency degradation (300-500% increase) is caused by **CPU resource contention via the shared TBB global thread pool**. Specifically:\n\n1. **Multiple components share TBB resources**: `StreamProcessor`, `BatchProcessor`, `ETLPipeline`, and `SchedulingService` all use TBB (Intel Threading Building Blocks) without explicit arena isolation.\n\n2. **Unbounded batch concurrency**: The ETLPipeline defaults to `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` (all CPU cores), allowing batch jobs to consume all available worker threads.\n\n3. **No prioritization mechanism**: The `ServiceManager` starts all services equally without resource boundaries, treating latency-sensitive real-time workloads the same as throughput-oriented batch jobs.\n\n### Key Components in Contention\n- **Real-time path**: `HL7MllpConnector` → `IngestionService` → `EventBusFacade` → `StreamProcessor` → `AlertingService`\n- **Batch path**: `SchedulingService` → `BatchProcessor` → `ETLPipeline` → `DataLakeFacade`\n\nBoth paths converge on the **global TBB thread pool**, causing the batch workload to starve the real-time path of CPU cycles during heavy ETL operations.\n\n### Proposed Solution: Workload-Aware Thread Pool Partitioning\nThe solution introduces dedicated, prioritized TBB `task_arena` instances:\n\n| Pool | Priority | Allocation | Components |\n|------|----------|------------|------------|\n| `realtime_pool` | High | 25% cores | StreamProcessor, AlertingService |\n| `ingestion_pool` | High | 15% cores | IngestionService |\n| `batch_pool` | Normal | 50% cores | ETLPipeline, BatchProcessor, SchedulingService |\n\n### Primary Files Requiring Changes\n1. `config/main_config.json.template` -
